Recovery: inference canary transient failure self-resolved within 74s (incident-20260729T164620-9c757def)

2026-07-29T16:48:16.453893Z · critical · recovery

The inference canary incident opened at 16:46:20Z and recovered at 16:47:34Z (~74 seconds). The canary probe returned an unexpected response ('None') but the model recovered without intervention. Recovery snapshot confirms full health: health endpoint returned 200 OK in 0.8ms, models endpoint returned 200 OK in 0.5ms, and vLLM served 29 total requests (23 stop, 6 length) with zero errors or aborts. GPU RTX 5090 at 35% utilization and 51C with no requests queued or running. Zero preemptions recorded. Active configuration matches known-good baseline with no diffs. Serve logs show all requests returning 200 OK throughout the incident window with no errors or warnings. No fatal signatures detected in logs. System resources healthy: 172GB RAM available, 79% disk free, load average 2.76/1.79/1.57. The last full verification at 16:34:49Z passed all checks including long-context retrieval. The canary failure appears to have been a transient glitch -- possibly a canary-side parsing issue, a brief generation anomaly, or a race condition in the inference pipeline. The model continued serving normally before, during, and after the incident window, and the system self-recovered without operator action.

Observations

  • Incident recovered at 16:47:34Z, ~74 seconds after opening at 16:46:20Z
  • Health endpoint returned 200 OK in 0.8ms at recovery time
  • Models endpoint returned 200 OK in 0.5ms, listing Qwen3.6-27B
  • vLLM served 29 total requests (23 stop, 6 length) with zero errors or aborts
  • Zero requests running or waiting at recovery snapshot
  • Zero preemptions recorded
  • GPU RTX 5090 at 35% utilization, 51C, no XID errors
  • Active configuration matches known-good baseline with no diffs
  • Serve logs show all 200 OK responses throughout incident window
  • No fatal signatures in logs
  • System load 2.76/1.79/1.57, 172GB RAM available, 79% disk free
  • Last full verification at 16:34:49Z passed all checks including long-context
  • Model continued serving normally before, during, and after the canary failure

Suggestions

  • Monitor the next scheduled canary and verification cycle to confirm sustained health
  • Review canary probe implementation for potential parsing edge cases that could produce 'None' responses
  • If canary failures recur, correlate with vLLM engine logs for brief errors not reflected in metrics
  • Consider increasing canary retry logic to distinguish transient glitches from sustained degradation
  • No immediate remediation needed -- the system self-recovered and is operating normally

Qwen3.6-27B steady on RTX 5090; canary probe returned None despite passing long-context verification

2026-07-29T16:47:29.314254Z · info · hourly

Snapshot at 16:46 UTC shows the appliance in steady serving mode with Qwen3.6-27B-NVFP4 on a single NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090 (Vast.ai). All verification checks passed: health endpoint 200 OK, short-prompt arithmetic/factual/instruction checks correct, structured output with reasoning field validated, and long-context retrieval at 32,768 tokens found all 3 needles across depths 0.15/0.55/0.9 in 8.0s. The active config matches the known-good baseline with zero drift. Metrics show 19 total requests since startup (15 stop, 4 length, 0 errors/preemptions/aborts). Mean TTFT ~2.4s, inter-token latency ~14.8ms, mean e2e latency ~9.3s. GPU at 43% utilization, 57C, 31.8GB/32.6GB VRAM used. System RAM 87% available, disk 79% free, load average 0.77 on 32 cores. The canary probe returned ok=false with response 'None' and 253ms latency, which is anomalous given the otherwise healthy state — likely a canary-specific timeout or parsing issue rather than a model problem, since the long-context probe (a more comprehensive check) succeeded in 16.1s. No fatal signatures in logs; only routine health/models/metrics GETs returning 200 OK.

Observations

  • Serving Qwen3.6-27B-NVFP4 on single RTX 5090, Vast.ai, HTTP-only port 8000 with API key auth
  • Verification suite fully passed: health, short prompt (3/3), structured output, long context 32K (3/3 needles)
  • Active config matches known-good baseline; zero drift since 16:38 UTC
  • 19 requests served: 15 stop, 4 length, 0 errors/preemptions/aborts
  • Mean TTFT ~2.4s; inter-token latency ~14.8ms; mean e2e ~9.3s across 19 requests
  • GPU 43% util, 57C, 31.8GB/32.6GB VRAM; system RAM 87% available; disk 79% free
  • Canary probe returned ok=false with response 'None' and 253ms latency
  • Long-context probe (separate, more comprehensive) succeeded in 16.1s with all checks passing
  • No fatal signatures in serve logs; only routine 200 OK health/models/metrics GETs
  • Load average 0.77 on 32-core host; 1 process running, 0 blocked

Suggestions

  • Investigate the canary probe failure (response 'None') — check if the canary endpoint or prompt format is misconfigured; the long-context probe passing suggests the model itself is healthy
  • Consider configuring TLS if the appliance will be exposed beyond a trusted network
  • Monitor whether the canary issue persists across subsequent hourly snapshots
  • The RTX 5090 lacks native FP4 compute; the Marlin kernel fallback may impact throughput on compute-heavy workloads — consider benchmarking against a non-FP4 variant if latency is a concern
  • Consider increasing gpu_memory_utilization above 0.9 if you want to reclaim CUDA graph memory overhead for more KV cache capacity
  • Review the DCP (decompression) settings if prefill latency on very long contexts becomes a bottleneck

Inference canary returned 'None' response on Qwen3.6-27B (incident-20260729T164620-9c757def)

2026-07-29T16:46:52.685283Z · critical · incident

At 2026-07-29T16:46:20Z, the inference canary probe failed against the Qwen3.6-27B model served on a single NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090. The canary received a response body of 'None' with 252.8ms latency, triggering a critical-severity incident. However, the broader appliance state is healthy: the health endpoint returned 200 OK (10.4ms), the models endpoint returned 200 OK (0.4ms), and a long-context probe completed successfully just 16 seconds earlier at 16:46:04Z with all checks passing (arithmetic, factual, instruction, structured output, and 32K-token needle-in-haystack retrieval). The last full verification at 16:34:49Z also passed all checks. GPU utilization is 43% at 57C, with no requests queued or running and zero error completions in vLLM metrics (19 total requests: 15 stop, 4 length). The active configuration matches the known-good baseline with no diffs. No fatal signatures were found in serve logs. The canary failure appears isolated and transient -- the model was serving normally moments before and after. The 'None' response may indicate a canary-side parsing issue, a brief model generation glitch, or a race condition in the inference pipeline rather than sustained model degradation.

False-positive OOM alert from synthetic chaos test; appliance healthy and serving normally

2026-07-29T16:43:54.786770Z · critical · incident

Incident incident-20260729T164321-d5d7c248 was opened with critical severity after the fatal-signature scanner detected 'CUDA out of memory' in serve-current.log. The triggering log line is explicitly prefixed with 'SOUL CHAOS TEST ONLY' and states 'Synthetic diagnostic event; vLLM remained healthy.' This is a deliberate chaos-test injection, not a genuine out-of-memory failure. Current system state confirms the appliance is healthy: the health endpoint returns 200 with 0.7ms latency, all verification checks passed (arithmetic, factual, instruction, structured output, and long-context retrieval at 32773 tokens), and the applyState is 'verified' in 'steady' mode with zero config drift from known-good. vLLM metrics show zero running or waiting requests, zero preemptions, and zero error completions. The served model is Qwen3.6-27B on a single NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090. GPU memory utilization is ~97.5% (31783/32607 MiB), consistent with the configured GPU_MEMORY_UTILIZATION=0.9. GPU temperature is 57C and utilization is 94%, both within normal operating ranges for a loaded inference appliance. System load averages are low (0.55/0.98/1.37) and filesystem has 79% free space. No evidence of actual memory pressure, request failures, or service degradation. The fatal signature scanner correctly matched the injected text pattern, but the event is a planned diagnostic exercise rather than a real incident. No operator action is required unless the chaos test framework is not expected to be active.

Observations

  • Log line triggering incident is prefixed 'SOUL CHAOS TEST ONLY' and labeled 'Synthetic diagnostic event'
  • Log explicitly states 'vLLM remained healthy' after the synthetic OOM message
  • Health endpoint returns HTTP 200 with 0.7ms latency
  • All verification checks passed: arithmetic, factual, instruction, structured output, long-context (32773 tokens, 3/3 needles found)
  • vLLM metrics: 0 running requests, 0 waiting, 0 preemptions, 0 error completions
  • applyState is 'verified' in 'steady' mode with empty diffFromKnownGood
  • GPU memory at 31783/32607 MiB (~97.5%), consistent with GPU_MEMORY_UTILIZATION=0.9
  • GPU temperature 57C, utilization 94% — normal for loaded inference
  • System load averages 0.55/0.98/1.37, filesystem 79% free
  • No request failures or latency anomalies in metrics

Suggestions

  • Confirm whether the chaos test framework is expected to be active; if not, investigate how it was triggered
  • Review chaos test configuration to ensure synthetic log injections do not produce unnecessary critical-severity incidents
  • Consider adding a filter to the fatal-signature scanner to exclude lines containing 'CHAOS TEST' or similar test markers
  • Verify that the chaos test injection did not cause any side effects beyond log pollution
  • Monitor GPU memory headroom during peak load to ensure the 0.9 utilization target remains safe under concurrent request bursts
